WA NO.709/2013 & Conn. cases
193
JUDGMENT
[WA Nos.709/2013, 427/2018, 2224/2018, 1101/2019,
1159/2019, 1317/2019 & W.P.(C)Nos.3818/2008,
8709/2008, 9797/2008, 10143/2008, 10409/2008,
14272/2008, 14273/2008, 14274/2008, 14278/2008,
16847/2008, 18290/2008, 23684/2008, 24690/2008,
27221/2008, 27644/2008, 27652/2008, 29183/2008,
30317/2008, 30539/2008, 31310/2008, 36917/2008,
37194/2008, 37520/2008, 1037/2009, 3615/2009,
7226/2009, 13206/2009, 15189/2009, 31213/2009,
31526/2009, 32563/2009, 32965/2009, 33496/2009,
34689/2009, 35595/2009, 35794/2009, 2188/2010,
3570/2010, 4765/2010, 6846/2010, 10153/2010,

19224/2010, 20317/2010, 23014/2010, 24749/2010,
24777/2010, 25866/2010, 30348/2010,32458/2010,

14761/2011, 16120/2011, 16983/2011, 17151/2011,

2004/2012, 8986/2013, 20950/2013, 7682/2014,
10641/2014, 11754/2014, 26024/2014, 23217/2015,
35038/2015,11950/2016,11951/2016, 14192/2016,
3240/2017,5290/2017, 29773/2017, 35784/2017,
37059/2017, 40557/2017, 19704/2018, 20394/2018,
25907/2018, 28934/2018, 29160/2018, 34518/2018,
35522/2018, 1379/2019, 2721/2019, 9532/2019,
33479/2019, 1490/2020, 4962/2020, 5610/2020,
17846/2020, 26683/2021, 4884/2022, 21623/2022,
22750/2022, 23576/2022, 26179/2022, 27380/2023,
32128/2023, 32131/2023,]

WA NO.709/2013 & Conn. cases
194
AMIT RAWAL, J.
1. This order of ours shall dispose of six writ appeals
and other writ petitions by a common order in view of the fact
that the question of law and facts are identical. All the
affected parties ie., the writ petitioners, invoked the writ
jurisdiction of this Court by laying challenge to the notices
issued by the District Executive Officer, Kerala Shops and
Commercial Establishments Welfare Fund Board on account of
non-payment of the dues in respect of the workers falling
under the category of 'worker' defined under the Kerala
Shops and Commercial Establishments Workers Welfare Fund
Act, 2006, ('2006 Act', for short). Essentially the notices were
challenged on the ground that the establishments were
covered under the provisions of various other Acts like
Employees Provident Fund and Miscellaneous Provisions Act,
1952, ESI Act etc. and in view of un-amended provisions of
2006 Act, there was an embargo upon the authorities under
the aforementioned Act for seeking payment of the welfare
fund. However, in view of the amendment caused in 2009 ie.,
by Act 5 of 2009 even if the establishments are covered

WA NO.709/2013 & Conn. cases
195
under the other provisions of the Act, but the one which is
registered under the Factories Act and Plantation Labour Act
alone have been exempted. In this view of the matter, they
would be liable to pay double the amount which would be
conducive for being a welfare scheme. Though they were
earlier registered under the Kerala Shops and Establishments
Act and had been paying fund when the amendment would be
causing a loss not monetary but otherwise also.
2. The amendment by Act 5 of 2009 has been made
effective six months from the date of its applicability ie., from
10.6.2008 without assigning the reasons, which has seriously
affected the rights of the workers, employers and employees.
It was also contended by learned counsel representing the
writ petitioners that in view of the provisions of Section 61 of
the Employees State Insurance Act 1942, there is a bar of
extending the benefits under the other Acts. In other words, if
a person is entitled to benefits under the ESI Act, he or she
shall not be entitle to as other similar benefits available under
the provisions of the Act.
3. In the writ appeals, challenge essentially is based

WA NO.709/2013 & Conn. cases
196
upon the fact that few of the judgment of the Single Bench
are based upon the applicability of law laid down in
Hymavathy v. Special Deputy Tahsildar [2008 (3) KLT
807] which has not taken into consideration the amendment
caused in 2009 and also in respect of the applicability of
provisions of the Kerala Motor Transport Workers Welfare
Fund Act, 1985, which is a different Code itself and would not
have any applicability vis-a-vis 2006 Act. Also, few of the
judgments have been passed only on account of the fact that
once the establishments are registered under the Employees
Provident Fund and Miscellaneous Provisions Act would not be
exigible and liable to pay the dues of the workers under 2006
Act.
4. Mr.Ahok.M.Cheriyan, learned Additional Advocate
General assisted by Mr.Vipindas and Smt.Sabeena P.Ismail,
learned Government Pleaders, have drawn the attention of
this Court to the amended and un-amended provisions of the
definition of 'workers' defined under Section 2(g), 2(h), 3A
and 4. Paragraphs 17, 18 and 26 of the Kerala Shops and
Commercial Establishments Welfare Scheme which provides

WA NO.709/2013 & Conn. cases
197
definition of the 'worker', the procedure for enrolling the
worker under the Welfare Fund Scheme and procedure for
registration etc. The applicability with retrospective effect
does not cause any burden upon any of the employees or
workers because it is not a mandate of the Act to pay the
amount under the provisions of the Act without enrollment.
5. We have heard the learned counsel for the parties and
appraised the paper books.
6. Un-amended Section 2(g), section 2(h) and Sections
3A and 4 are extracted hereinbelow:
2(g) "Worker" means an employee coming under the
purview of the Kerala Shops and Commercial Establishments
Act, 1960 (34 of 1960) and includes a person employed in
Establishments including hospitals, nursing home, ayurvedic
pharmacy, dispensary, medical shop, paramedical
institution, parcel service, petrol-diesel autogas bunks, fish
processing establishment, food processing establishment,
textile manufacturing establishment, hotel, restaurant, meat
shop, computer-computer allied service establishment,
printing press, telephone booth, courier service, cooking gas
distribution agency, hostel, hill produce-procurement and
marketing establishment, small scale copra processing unit,
small scale oil mill, leather procurement establishment, small
scale foot wear-bag manufacturing establishment, cinema
theatre, photo/video studio, bakery, automobile-engineering
establishment, establishment providing sound, light and

WA NO.709/2013 & Conn. cases
198
decoration or in trade/commercial establishments and who
receives wages from the employer directly or otherwise or
through contractor or agent or by agreement or otherwise or
a self employed person and any other person, declared by
the Government from time to time, by notification in the
Gazette, for the purposes of this Act to be an employee
coming under the purview of the Kerala Shops and
Commercial Establishments Act:
Substituted by Act 5 of 2009 in K. G. Ext. No. 37 dt.
05/01/2009 (w.e.f. 10/06/2008). Prior to the substitution it
read as:
"Provided that the employees coming under the purview of the
Factories Act, 1948 (Central Act 63 of 1948) or the Plantation
Labour Act, 1951 (Central Act 69 of 1951) or those working in
the Establishments where any other Welfare Fund of the
Government of Kerala, are applicable and in Establishments
exempted by the Government, from the provisions of the
Kerala Shops and Commercial Establishments Act, 1960 and
those who are getting benefit under the purview of any other
law, for the time being in force, shall not be included in this
Welfare Fund;"
(h) "member" means a member of the Fund;
"3A. A worker who is a member of the Kerala
Labour Welfare Fund and coming under the purview of
the Kerala Shops and Commercial Establishments Act
be a member of the Fund. –(1) Notwithstanding anything
contained in the Kerala Labour Welfare Fund Act, 1975 (11
of 1977), a worker who is a member of the Kerala Labour
Welfare Fund and coming under the purview of the Kerala
Shops and Commercial Establishments Act, 1960 ( 34 of

WA NO.709/2013 & Conn. cases
199
1960) shall be a worker coming under the purview of this
Act and shall have to become member of the Welfare Fund
constituted under this Act.
(2) A worker who became member under sub-section
(1) shall cease to be a member of the Labour Welfare Fund
under the Kerala Labour Welfare Fund Act, 1975."
4. Contribution to the Fund.—(l) Every member shall
contribute to the Fund rupees twenty per month.
(2) Every employer shall contribute, to the Fund
rupees twenty per month, in respect of each worker
employed by him.
(3) A self employed person shall pay rupees twenty
every month in addition to the contribution to the Fund as
per sub-section (1).
(4) The 'Government shall contribute to the Fund
by way of grant, an amount of rupees five per month paid by
each member under sub-section (1) or , twenty five per cent
of the employee's contribution; whichever is higher.
(5) The amount of contribution to be remitted as
such shall be deposited in a Co-operative Bank recognised
by the Board, or Nationalized Banks or any institutions
decided by the Government; for collecting such amount.
(6) The Government may, by notification in the
Gazette, revise the rate of contribution specified in sub-section (1) and (2) and the rate of grant specified in sub-section (4), once in every three years taking into account
the expenses required for the implementation of the
scheme,
(7) Every notification under sub-section (6), shall be laid
as soon as may be, after it is issued, before the Legislative

WA NO.709/2013 & Conn. cases
200
Assembly while it is in session for a total period of fourteen
days, which may be comprised in one session or in two
successive sessions and if, before the expiry of the session
in which it is so laid or the session immediately following,
the Legislative Assembly makes any modification in the
notification or decides that the notification should not be
issued, the notification shall, thereafter, have effect only in
such modified form or be of no effect, as the case may be;
so however that any such modification or annulment shall be
without prejudice to the validity of anything previously done
under that notification.
7. Section 3A of the Act amended while causing the
proviso to Section 2(g) envisage that for the purpose of this
Act, a worker should be a member of Kerala Labour Welfare
Fund coming under the purview of Kerala Shops and
Commercial Establishment Act, 1960, and also under the
provision of Kerala Shops and Commercial Establishment
2006 Act shall have to become a member of the welfare fund
constituted under this Act. What is deduced from the perusal
of the aforementioned Section is, until and unless an
employer or an employee is not enrolled as a member of the
welfare fund, would not be liable to pay the contribution
under the provisions of 1960 or 2006 Act. Section 4

WA NO.709/2013 & Conn. cases
201
prescribes the procedure for the contribution of the fund ie.,
the amount vis-a-vis the employer and employees. Paragraph
17 of the Scheme prescribes the procedure of eligibility for
membership, 18 prescribes the procedure whereas 26, the
procedure for registration. Until and unless these conditions
are not complied with employee/worker or an employer would
not be liable to give/extend the benefit under the provisions
of 1960 (2006) Act and the scheme framed thereunder.
8. The question now which has been raised is whether
the notices sent by the competent authority under the Shops
and Commercial Establishment Workers Welfare Fund are
legal and justified in view of the amended and un-amended
provisions. Therefore, the question raised in the writ petitions
and writ appeals is answered from the perusal of the
aforementioned provisions. For the sake of repetition, until
and unless procedure prescribed extracted above therein and
discussed is not followed, liability would not accrue either
under 1960 or 2006 Act and the scheme framed thereunder.
Even the question of retrospective effect would not arise until
and unless the procedure of registration for becoming or

WA NO.709/2013 & Conn. cases
202
enrolling as a member of the fund is not followed. The
grievance of the petitioners challenging the vires of the Act is
also answered in their favour for the reason that in view of
the contention raised by the counsel representing the workers
to the extent that in case the benefits have been derived
under the provisions of a particular Act, the benefit of other
Acts like 2006 and other provisions of the Act would not be
extended. Thus we are of the view that the authorities under
the Act have not undertaken an exercise for ascertaining the
applicability of the provisions to the various establishments
and in the absence of the same, could not have sent the
notices in a cavalier manner.
9. Thus we allow the writ petitions by quashing the
demand and allow the writ appeals by setting aside the
judgment of the Single Bench with the following directions:
1. The authorities established under 2006 Act will
undertake a task of making an enquiry before issuing
the notices regarding the applicability or non-applicability of the Act and also the provisions of
various other Acts like Section 61 of the Employees

WA NO.709/2013 & Conn. cases
203
State Insurance Act, 1948.
2. An opportunity hearing would be given. Parties
affected would be at liberty to show to authority as
to the applicability or coverage of 2006 Act and
scheme framed thereunder. In other words, until and
unless the workers or the employer himself are not
enrolled as a welfare fund would not be covered
under the provisions of 2006 Act. Since the
amendment has made effective from 10.06.2008, it
would not seriously affect the establishments
registered under the Act for, beneficiaries under
2006 Act would not be entitled to claim double
benefit vis-a-vis the Act in question and other
provisions of the Act.
Sd/-
AMIT RAWAL
JUDGE
Sd/-
EASWARAN S.
JUDGE
nak

WA NO.709/2013 & Conn. cases
204
